T_{\mathsf{SBA}}
.

3.3
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
: Asynchronous BA with Synchronous Validity

To the best of our knowledge, the
only
known
unconditionally-secure
ABA protocol is due to
[
23
]
, which generalizes the framework of
randomized ABA
[
51
,
11
,
21
,
31
]
against general adversaries. The protocol of
[
23
]
uses a
graded agreement
(GA) protocol (also known as the vote protocol),
along with a secure coin-flip protocol. Unfortunately, both these primitives provide security (in an
asynchronous
network) against
\mathbb{Q}^{(3)}
adversary structures
and
fail
to provide any security guarantees against
\mathbb{Q}^{(2)}
adversary structures in a
synchronous
network. Consequently, the ABA protocol of
[
23
]

fails

to provide any security guarantees in a
synchronous
network against
\mathbb{Q}^{(2)}
adversary structures.
11
11

11

Recall that we need
validity
, coupled with
guaranteed liveness
from

\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
, when used in our network agnostic BA protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
We give a different instantiation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
by generalizing a few ideas used in
[
15
]
. Our instantiation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
is based
on following
two
components.

Component I: Asynchronous Graded Agreement with Synchronous Validity.

We assume the existence of a GA protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, where each party has a binary input. The output for each party is a value from
\{0,1,\bot\}
, along with a grade from
\{0,1,2\}
.
The protocol achieves the following properties.

–

Asynchronous Network
— The following properties are achieved, even if the adversary corrupts any subset from
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
:
(a):
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Liveness:
If all honest parties participate in the protocol, then each honest party eventually obtains an output.

(b)
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Graded Validity:
If every honest party’s input is
b
, then all honest parties with an output, output
(b,2)
.

(c)
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Graded Consistency:
If two honest parties output grades
g,g^{\prime}
, then
|g-g^{\prime}|\leq 1
holds; moreover,
if two honest parties output
(v,g)
and
(v^{\prime},g^{\prime})
with
g,g^{\prime}\geq 1
, then
v=v^{\prime}
.

–

Synchronous Network
— The following properties are achieved, even if the adversary corrupts any subset from
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
:
(a):
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Liveness:

If all honest parties participate in the protocol with the
same
input, then after some fixed time
T_{\mathsf{GA}}
,
all honest parties obtain an output.

(b)
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Graded Validity:
If every honest party’s input is
b
, then all honest parties with an output, output
(b,2)
.

Note that we
do not
require any form of consistency from
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
in the
synchronous
network.
We give an instantiation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
with the
\mathbb{Q}^{(2,1)}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a})
condition, by generalizing the
threshold
GA protocol of
[
15
]
with condition
2t_{s}+t_{a}<n
, such that

T_{\mathsf{GA}}=4\cdot\Delta
.
The protocol of
[
15
]
uses digital signatures (hence, is
computationally secure
),
which we replace with pseudo-signatures.
For the description of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
and its properties, see Appendix
0.A
.

Component II: Asynchronous Coin-Flipping with Synchronous Liveness.

We assume the existence of a
p
-coin-flipping protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
, where
0<p<1
is a parameter.
In the protocol, the parties participate with random inputs and the output of each party is a bit satisfying the following properties.

–

Asynchronous Network
: The following properties are achieved even if the adversary corrupts any subset from
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
:
(a):
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Almost-Surely Liveness:

If all honest parties participate in the protocol, then almost-surely, all honest parties eventually get an output.

(b):
(\mathcal{Z}_{a},p)
-Commonness:
With probability
p
, the output of
all
honest parties is a
random
bit
b\in\{0,1\}
.

–

Synchronous Network
: The following property is achieved even if the adversary corrupts any subset from
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
:
(a):
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Guaranteed Liveness:

If all honest parties participate in the protocol, then all honest parties get an output, after some
fixed
time
T_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
.

In
[
23
]
, the authors presented an instantiation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
, which achieves
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Almost-Surely Liveness
as well as
(\mathcal{Z}_{a},p)
-Commonness
in an asynchronous network,
where
p=\frac{1}{n}
, provided
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(3)}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{a})
condition. The protocol incurs an
expected
communication of
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{poly}(n,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|,\log{|\mathbb{F}|}))
bits.
Interestingly, the protocol also achieves
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Guaranteed Liveness
in a
synchronous
network,
irrespective
of
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
.
Namely, after time
T_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}=20\cdot\Delta
, all honest parties will have an output,
with (honest)
parties communicating
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{poly}(n,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|,\log{|\mathbb{F}|}))
bits.

3.3.1
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}+\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}\rightarrow\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}

Once we have instantiations of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
, we can easily combine it using the framework of
[
51
,
11
,
21
,
31
]
to get the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
.
The protocol consists of several iterations, where in each iteration, the parties run two instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, along with an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
.
Using the first instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, the parties check if they all have the same input. Independent of this finding, they then run an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
. Finally, they again run an instance of

\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, with inputs being carefully chosen. Namely, if a party obtained an output with the
highest
grade from the
first
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, then it participates with this input,
else it participates with the coin-output. Finally, based on the output received from the second instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, the parties update their input for the next iteration as follows:
if a bit with a non-zero grade is obtained, then it is set as the updated input, else the updated input is set to the input of the second instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
.
During each iteration, the parties keep a tab on whether they have received an output bit with the
highest
grade from the
second
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, in which case, they
indicate it to the others by sending a signed
\mathsf{ready}
message and the bit. Once “sufficiently many” parties send the same signed
\mathsf{ready}
bit, it is taken as the output of the protocol.

The idea here is that if the honest parties start an iteration with the
same
input bit
b
, then the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
is
not
considered
(
irrespective
of the network type) and
all
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
output
(b,2)
. Thus, all honest parties will send a signed
\mathsf{ready}
message for
b
. Consequently, all honest parties will output
b
.
This ensures
validity
, coupled with
guaranteed liveness
, both in
synchronous
and
asynchronous
networks.
On the other hand, if the honest parties start an iteration with
different
inputs, then with probability at least
p\cdot\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{2}
, all of them will have the

same
input for the second instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
. And consequently, all honest parties will have the same input from the next iteration onward and consistency is achieved
(in the
asynchronous
network).
The description of
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}
based on
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
is recalled from
[
23
]
and presented in Fig
5
.

\bullet

Initialisation
: Set
b=b_{i}
,
\mathsf{committed}=\mathsf{false}
and
k=1
. Then do the
following.

1.

Participate in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
protocol with input
b
and
wait for time
T_{\mathsf{GA}}
.

2.

Once an output
(b,g)
is received from the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
, participate in an
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
and
wait for time
T_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
. Let
\mathsf{Coin}_{k}
denote the output received from
\Pi_{\mathsf{CoinFlip}}
.

3.

If
g<2
, then set
b=\mathsf{Coin}_{k}
.

4.

Participate in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{GA}}
protocol with input
b
and
wait for time
T_{\mathsf{GA}}
. Let
(b^{\prime},g^{\prime})
be
the output received. If
g^{\prime}>0
, then set
b=b^{\prime}
.

5.

If
g^{\prime}=2
and
\mathsf{committed}=\mathsf{false}
, then set
\mathsf{committed}=\mathsf{true}
and send

\langle(\mathsf{ready},b)\rangle_{i}
to all the parties.

6.

Set
k=k+1
and repeat from
1
.

\bullet

Output Computation
:

–

Upon receiving
\langle(\mathsf{ready},b)\rangle_{j}
messages with valid signatures corresponding to every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}\setminus Z
for some
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, send

(b,\mathcal{C}(b))
to all the parties and
output
b
. Here
\mathcal{C}(b)
denotes the collection of signed
\langle(\mathsf{ready},b)\rangle_{j}
messages.

–

Upon receiving
(b,\mathcal{C}(b))
where
\mathcal{C}(b)
has valid signatures corresponding to every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}\setminus Z
for some
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
,
send
(b,\mathcal{C}(b))
to all the parties and
output
b
.

Figure 5
:
The ABA protocol. The above code is executed by every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
with input
b_{i}

]

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{ABA}}

4
Network Agnostic Information Checking Protocol

In this section, we present our network agnostic ICP protocol (Fig
6
). A detailed overview of the protocol has been already presented in Section
1.2.2
.
The protocol consists of two subprotocols
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, implementing the authentication and revelation phase respectively,
where the parties participate in the revelation phase only upon completing the authentication phase.
During the authentication phase,
\mathsf{S}
distributes the authentication and verification information, followed by parties publicly verifying the consistency of distributed information and once the consistency is established, the
authentication phase is over. During the revelation phase,
\mathsf{I}
reveals the IC-signature which is verified by
\mathsf{R}
with respect to the verification information revealed by a “selected” subset of the verifiers.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a},\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
:
t\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\max\{|Z|:Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}\}
\justify
\justify

–

Distributing Data
:
\mathsf{S}
executes the following steps.

\bullet

Randomly select
t
-degree
signing-polynomial

F(x)
and
t
-degree
masking-polynomial

M(x)
,
where
F(0)=s
.
For
i=1,\ldots,n
, randomly select
\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{F}\setminus\{0\}
, and compute

v_{i}=F(\alpha_{i})
and
m_{i}=M(\alpha_{i})
.

\bullet

Send
(F(x),M(x))
to
\mathsf{I}
. For
i=1,\ldots,n
,
send
(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
to party
P_{i}
.

–

Confirming Receipt of Verification Points
: Each party
P_{i}
(including
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
), upon receiving
(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
from
\mathsf{S}
, broadcasts
(\mathsf{Received},i)
.

–

Announcing Set of Supporting Verifiers
: only
\mathsf{S}
does the following.

\bullet

Initialize the set of
supporting verifiers

\mathcal{SV}
to
\emptyset
, and
wait till the local time is
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Upon receiving
(\mathsf{Received},i)
from the broadcast of
P_{i}

add
P_{i}
to
\mathcal{SV}
. Once
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{SV}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}

\mathcal{SV}
.

–

Announcing Masked Polynomial
: only
\mathsf{I}
does the following.

\bullet

Wait till the local time is
\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Upon receiving
\mathcal{SV}

\mathsf{S}
such that
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{SV}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}

d\in\mathbb{F}\setminus\{0\}
and broadcast
(d,B(x))
, where
B(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}dF(x)+M(x)
.

–

Announcing Validity of Masked Polynomial
: only
\mathsf{S}
does the following.

\bullet

Wait till the local time is
\Delta+3T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Upon receiving
(d,B(x))
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{I}
,
broadcast
\mathsf{OK}
, if
B(x)
is a
t
-degree polynomial and if

dv_{j}+m_{j}=B(\alpha_{j})
holds
for every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{SV}
.

–

Deciding Whether Authentication is Successful
: each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
(including
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
)
waits till the local time is
\Delta+4T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Upon receiving
\mathcal{SV}
and
(d,B(x))
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{S}
and
\mathsf{I}
respectively, where
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{SV}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
,
it set the variable
\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}
to
1
if
\mathsf{OK}
is received from the broadcast of
\mathsf{S}
.
Upon setting
\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}
to
1
,
\mathsf{I}
sets

\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)=F(x)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a},\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)

–

Revealing Signing Polynomial and Verification Points
: Each party
P_{i}
(including
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
)

\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}

1
.

\bullet

If
P_{i}=\mathsf{I}

F(x)
to

during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.

\bullet

If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{SV}

(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
to
\mathsf{R}
.

Accepting the IC-Signature
: The following steps are executed only by
\mathsf{R}
, if

\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}
is set to
1
during the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.

Wait till the local time becomes a multiple of
\Delta
.

F(x)

v_{i}=F(\alpha_{i})

\mathsf{I}
during

(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
.

is found, such that
\mathcal{SV}\setminus\mathcal{SV}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
,
and for every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{SV}^{\prime}
, the corresponding revealed point

is

. Then, output
s=F(0)
.

.

Theorem 4.1

Protocols
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}},\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}})
satisfy the following properties, except with
probability at most
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\frac{nt}{|\mathbb{F}|-1}
, where

t=\max\{|Z|:Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}\}
.

–

If
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
are honest, then the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Correctness
: In a synchronous network, each honest party sets
\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}

to
1
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
at time
T_{\mathsf{Auth}}=\Delta+4T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Moreover
\mathsf{R}
outputs
s
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
which takes
T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}=\Delta
time.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Correctness
: In an asynchronous network, each honest
party eventually sets
\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})}

to
1
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and
\mathsf{R}
eventually outputs
s
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.

–

Privacy
: The view of
\mathsf{Adv}
is independent of
s
, irrespective of the network.

–

Unforgeability
: If
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{R}
are honest,
\mathsf{I}
is corrupt
and if
\mathsf{R}
outputs
s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, then
s^{\prime}=s
holds, irrespective of the network type.

–

If
\mathsf{S}
is corrupt,
\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R}
are honest
and if
\mathsf{I}
sets
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)=F(x)
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
, then the following holds.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Non-Repudiation
: In a synchronous network,
\mathsf{R}
outputs
s=F(0)
during during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, which takes
T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}=\Delta
time.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Non-Repudiation
: In an asynchronous network,
\mathsf{R}
eventually outputs
s=F(0)
during during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.

–

Communication Complexity
: Irrespective of the network type,
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
incurs a communication of
\mathcal{O}(n^{5}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits, while

\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
incurs a communication of
\mathcal{O}(n\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|})
bits.

Looking ahead, in our VSS protocols, there will be several instances of ICP running, with different parties playing the role of
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
. It will be convenient to use the following notations while invoking instances of
ICP.

Notation 4.2
(for ICP)

While using
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}},\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}})
, we will say that:

–

“
P_{i}
gives
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s)
to
P_{j}
” to mean that
P_{i}
acts as
\mathsf{S}
and invokes an instance of

\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
with input
s
, where
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
play the role of
\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
respectively.

–

“
P_{j}
receives
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s)
from
P_{i}
” to mean that

P_{j}
, as
\mathsf{I}
, has set

\mathsf{authCompleted}_{(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k})}
to
1

and
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s)
to some
t
-degree polynomial with
s
as the constant term
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
, where
P_{i}
and
P_{k}

play the role of
\mathsf{S}
and
\mathsf{R}
respectively.

–

“
P_{j}
reveals
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s)
to
P_{k}
” to mean
P_{j}
, as
\mathsf{I}
, invokes an instance
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, with
P_{i}
and
P_{k}
playing the role of
\mathsf{S}

and
\mathsf{R}
respectively.

–

“
P_{k}
accepts
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s)
” to mean that

P_{k}
, as
\mathsf{R}
, outputs
s
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,
invoked by
P_{j}
as
\mathsf{I}
, with
P_{i}
playing the role of
\mathsf{S}
.

4.1
Linearity of IC Signature

Our ICP satisfies the
linearity
property, provided “special care” is taken while generating the IC-signatures. In more detail,
consider a
fixed

\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I}
and
\mathsf{R}
and let
s_{a}
and
s_{b}
be two values, such that
\mathsf{I}
has received
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{a})
and

\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{b})
from
\mathsf{S}
,
through instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(a)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(b)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
respectively, where
all
the following conditions are satisfied.

–

Supporting verifiers
\mathcal{SV}_{a}
and
\mathcal{SV}_{b}
, during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(a)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(b)}
, are the
same
.

–

For
i=1,\ldots,n
, corresponding to the verifier
P_{i}
,
\mathsf{S}
has used the
same

\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{F}\setminus\{0\}
, to compute the verification points, during

\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(a)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(b)}

–

\mathsf{I}
has used the
same
linear combiner
d\in\mathbb{F}\setminus\{0\}
during the instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(a)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(b)}
, to compute the linearly-combined masked polynomials.

Let
s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}c_{1}\cdot s_{a}+c_{2}\cdot s_{b}
, where
c_{1},c_{2}
are
publicly known
constants from
\mathbb{F}
. It then follows that if all the above conditions are satisfied, then
\mathsf{I}

can
locally
compute
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
from
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{a})
and
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{b})
.
Namely,
\mathsf{I}
can set
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)=c_{1}\cdot\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{a})+c_{2}\cdot\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{b})
.
On the other hand, let the verifier
P_{i}\in\mathcal{SV}_{a}
hold the verification points
(\alpha_{i},v_{a,i},m_{a,i})
and
(\alpha_{i},v_{b,i},m_{b,i})
, corresponding to

\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{a})
and
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s_{b})
respectively. Then

P_{i}
can
locally
compute
(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
as its verification point corresponding to
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
, where

v_{i}=c_{1}\cdot v_{a,i}+c_{2}\cdot v_{b,i}
and
m_{i}=c_{1}\cdot m_{a,i}+c_{2}\cdot m_{b,i}
. During the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,
to reveal
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
,
the intermediary
\mathsf{I}

can reveal
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
to
\mathsf{R}
, while each verifier
P_{i}\in\mathcal{SV}_{a}
can reveal the verification information
(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
.
To accept
(\alpha_{i},v_{i},m_{i})
, the receiver
\mathsf{R}
either checks for the “consistency” of
(\alpha_{i},v_{i})
with
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
,
or the “inconsistency” of masked polynomial
B(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}c_{1}\cdot B_{a}(x)+c_{2}\cdot B_{b}(x)
with
(d,v_{i},m_{i})
; here

B_{a}(x)
and
B_{b}(x)
denote the masked polynomials, made public by
\mathsf{I}
, during the instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(a)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}^{(b)}
respectively,
both
computed with respect to the linear
combiner
d
.

Looking ahead, we will require the linearity property from ICP, when used in our VSS protocols, where there will be multiple instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
running, involving the
same

(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})
triplet.
To achieve this, we will ensure that in all the
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
instances invoked during VSS
involving the
same
triplet
(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})
, the signer uses the same non-zero evaluation point
\alpha_{\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},i}
for the verifier
P_{i}
, while distributing
verification information to
P_{i}
, as part of the respective
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
instances. Similarly,
\mathsf{S}
should find and
make public a
common
set of supporting verifiers
\mathcal{SV}
, on behalf of
all
the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.
And finally,
\mathsf{I}
should use the same non-zero random linear combiner
d
, to compute the masked polynomials for all the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and once computed, it should
together

make public

d
and the masked polynomials for
all
the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.

In the rest of the paper, we will use the term “
parties follow linearity principle while generating IC-signatures
”, to mean
that the underlying instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
are invoked as above.

4.2
Default IC Signature

In our VSS protocols, we will also encounter situations where some
publicly known
value
s
and a triplet
(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R})
exist. Then
\mathsf{I}
can

locally
compute
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
by setting
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
to the
constant
polynomial
F(x)=s
.
Each
verifier
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

locally
sets
(\alpha_{\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},i},v_{i},m_{i})
as its verification information, where
v_{i}=m_{i}=s
.
Moreover, the set of supporting verifiers
\mathcal{SV}
is set as
\mathcal{P}
.
Notice that the way in which
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
and the verification information is set guarantees that, later, if an
honest

\mathsf{I}
reveals

\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
to an
honest

\mathsf{R}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, then
\mathsf{R}

always
outputs
s
.

In the rest of the paper, we will use the term “
parties set
\mathsf{ICSig}(\mathsf{S},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{R},s)
to the default value
”, to mean the above.

5
Network Agnostic Verifiable Secret Sharing (VSS)

This section presents our network-agnostic VSS protocol, which allows a designated dealer to generate a linear secret-sharing with IC-signatures for its input. We first define the notion of linear
secret-sharing with IC-signatures.

Definition 3
(Linear Secret Sharing with IC-Signatures)

A value
s\in\mathbb{F}
is said to be linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, if there exist shares
s_{1},\ldots,s_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}\in\mathbb{F}
where
s=s_{1}+\ldots+s_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
. Moreover, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
,
there exists some publicly-known
core-set

\mathcal{W}_{q}\subseteq S_{q}
, such that
all
the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition and
all (honest) parties in the set
S_{q}
have the share
s_{q}
.

–

Every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
has the IC-signature

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
for every
P_{k}\not\in S_{q}
.
Moreover, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
has
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{\prime}_{q})
of any honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
for any
P_{k}\not\in S_{q}
, then

s^{\prime}_{q}=s_{q}
holds. Furthermore, all the underlying IC-signatures satisfy the linearity property.

The vector of information corresponding to a linear secret-sharing with IC-signature of
s
is denoted by
[s]
, which includes the share
s_{1},\ldots,s_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, the core sets
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}

and IC-signatures
\{\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s_{q})\}_{P_{j},P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q},P_{k}\not\in S_{q}}
. For convenience, we denote the
q^{th}
share of
s
, corresponding to
S_{q}
, by

[s]_{q}
.

A vector of values
\vec{S}=(s^{(1)},\ldots,s^{(L)})
where
L\geq 1
is said to be linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, if each
s^{(\ell)}\in\vec{S}
is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures
and if there exist common core sets
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, corresponding to the secret-sharings
[s^{(1)}],\ldots,[s^{(\ell)}]
.

If
\vec{S}=(s^{(1)},\ldots,s^{(L)})
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, then the parties can
locally
compute any publicly-known function of these secret-shared values.
In more detail, let

c_{1},\ldots,c_{L}\in\mathbb{F}
be publicly-known constants and let
s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}c_{1}\cdot s^{(1)}+\ldots+c_{L}\cdot s^{(L)}
.
Then
the following holds:

c_{1}\cdot[s^{(1)}]+\ldots+c_{L}\cdot[s^{(L)}]=[s],

where the core-sets corresponding to
[s]
are

\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.
And corresponding to each
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, the share
[s]_{q}

for each (honest) party in
S_{q}
can be computed locally as
c_{1}\cdot[s^{(1)}]_{q}+\ldots+c_{\ell}\cdot[s^{(\ell)}]_{q}
.
Moreover, every (honest)
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
can compute the IC-signature
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})

of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
for every
P_{k}\not\in S_{q}
, from
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s^{(1)}]_{q}),\ldots,\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s^{(\ell)}]_{q})
.
In the rest of the paper, we will say that the “
parties in
{\mathcal{}P}

locally compute
[c_{1}\cdot s^{(1)}+\ldots+c_{\ell}\cdot s^{(\ell)}]
from
[s^{(1)}],\ldots,[s^{(\ell)}]
” to mean the above.

5.1
The VSS Protocol

We present a network-agnostic VSS protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
(Fig
7
). In the protocol, there exists a designated
dealer

\mathsf{D}\in\mathcal{P}
with input
s
(the protocol can be easily generalized if

\mathsf{D}
has
L
inputs). The protocol allows
\mathsf{D}
to “verifiably” generate a linear secret-sharing of

s
with IC-signatures. In a
synchronous
network, the (honest) parties output

[s]
after a “fixed” time, while in an
asynchronous
network, they do so
eventually
, such that

s
remains private. The verifiability here guarantees that if
\mathsf{D}
is
corrupt
and some honest party gets an output, then there exists some value, say
s^{\star}
(which could be different from
s
),
such that
s^{\star}
is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. Note that in this case, we
cannot
bound the time within which
s^{\star}
will be secret-shared, since a potentially
corrupt

\mathsf{D}
may delay sending the
required messages and the parties will
not
be knowing the exact network type. A detailed overview of the protocol has been already presented in Section
1.2.3
and so we directly present the protocol.

\justify

–

Distribution of Shares
:
\mathsf{D}
,
on having input
s
, randomly chooses
s_{1},\dots,s_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}\in\mathbb{F}
,
such that
s=s_{1}+\dots+s_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
. It then sends
s_{q}
to all
P_{i}\in S_{q}
, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.

–

Exchanging IC-Signed Values
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
(including
\mathsf{D}
),
waits till the local time becomes
\Delta
.
Then, for each
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
such that
P_{i}\in S_{q}
,
upon receiving
s_{qi}
from
\mathsf{D}
, give
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s_{qi})
to every
P_{j}\in S_{q}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, such that
the parties
follow the linearity principle while generating IC-signatures
(see Section
4.1
).

–

Announcing Results of Pairwise Consistency Tests
:
Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
(including
\mathsf{D}
)
waits till the local time becomes
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}

and then does the following.

–

Upon receiving
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s_{qj})
from
P_{j}
for each
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}
such that
P_{j},P_{i}\in S_{q}
, corresponding to every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
,
broadcast
\mathsf{OK}(i,j)
, if

s_{qi}=s_{qj}
holds.

–

Corresponding to every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}
, participate in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
initiated by
P_{j}
as a sender, to broadcast any
\mathsf{OK}(P_{j},\star)
message.

–

Constructing Consistency Graph
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
(including
\mathsf{D}
)
waits till the local time becomes
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}

and then constructs an undirected consistency graph
G^{(i)}
with
\mathcal{P}
as the vertex set, where
the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is added to
G^{(i)}
, provided
\mathsf{OK}(j,k)
and
\mathsf{OK}(k,j)
is received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively (through any mode).

–

Identification of Core Sets and Public Announcement by the Dealer
:
\mathsf{D}

waits till its local time is
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, and then
executes the following steps to compute core sets.

–

Once any
S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}

forms a clique in the graph
G^{(\mathsf{D})}
, then for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, compute core-set
\mathcal{W}_{q}

and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}
with respect to
S_{p}
as follows, followed by broadcasting

(\mathsf{CanCS},\mathsf{D},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}_{q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS},\{s_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}})
.
12
12

12

If there are multiple
S_{p}
from
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
which
constitute a clique in
G^{(\mathsf{D})}
, then consider the one with the smallest index.

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\mathsf{D})}
, then set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\mathsf{D})}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition,
then set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=S_{q}
and include
q
to
\mathcal{BS}
.

–

Identifying Valid Core Sets
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits till its local time is
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
and then initializes a set
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\emptyset
. For

p=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, party
P_{i}
includes
(\mathsf{D},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
(initialized to
\emptyset
), provided
all
the following hold.

–

(\mathsf{CanCS},\mathsf{D},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}_{q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS},\{s_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}})

is received from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, such that for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, the following hold.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}
, then the set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=S_{q}
.

–

If
(q\not\in\mathcal{BS})
, then
\mathcal{W}_{q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, such that:

–

If
\mathcal{W}_{q}=S_{q}
, then
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in
G^{(i)}
.

–

Else if
\mathcal{W}_{q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, then

(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(i)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

Computing Output
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following, once
\mathcal{C}_{i}\neq\emptyset
.

–

For every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
such that
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, reveal
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})
to every
P_{k}\in\mathsf{S}_{q}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
upon computing
[s]_{q}
and
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})
as follows.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}
, then set
[s]_{q}=s_{q}
, where
s_{q}
is received from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
,
as part of the message

(\mathsf{CanCS},\mathsf{D},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}_{q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS},\{s_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}})
. Moreover, for every
P_{j}\in S_{q}
and every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, set

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},\allowbreak P_{k},[s]_{q})
to the default value.

–

Else, set
[s]_{q}
to
s_{qi}
, where
s_{qi}
was received from
\mathsf{D}
. Moreover, for every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, set

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})
to
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s_{qj})
, received from
P_{j}
.

–

For every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
such that
P_{i}\in S_{q}\setminus W_{q}
, compute
[s]_{q}
as follows.

–

Check if there exists any
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and a value
s_{qj}
, such that
P_{i}
has accepted

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},s_{qj})
, corresponding to every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
. Upon finding such a
P_{j}
,
set
[s]_{q}=s_{qj}
.

–

Wait till the local time becomes
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
. Upon setting
\{[s]_{q}\}_{P_{i}\in S_{q}}
to some value, output
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,

\{[s]_{q}\}_{P_{i}\in S_{q}}
and
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})_{P_{j},P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q},P_{k}\not\in S_{q}}
.

Figure 7
:
The network agnostic VSS protocol

The properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
as stated in Theorem
5.1
are proved in Appendix
0.C
.

Theorem 5.1

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
achieves the following, except with a probability of
\mathcal{O}(|\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}|\cdot n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, where
\mathsf{D}
has input
s\in\mathbb{F}
for
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
and where

T_{\mathsf{VSS}}=\Delta+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is honest, then the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
: In a synchronous network, the honest parties output
[s]
at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: In an asynchronous network, the honest parties eventually output
[s]
.

–

Privacy
: Adversary’s view remains independent of
s
in any network.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt, then the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-commitment
: In a synchronous network, either no honest party computes any output or there exists some

s^{\star}\in\mathbb{F}
, such that the honest parties output
[s^{\star}]
.
Moreover, if any honest party computes its output at time
T
, then all honest parties
compute their required output by time
T+\Delta
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-commitment
: In an asynchronous network, either no honest party computes any output or there exists some

s^{\star}\in\mathbb{F}
, such that the honest parties eventually output
[s^{\star}]
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot n^{8}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits are communicated by the honest parties.

6
Network Agnostic Reconstruction Protocols and Secure Verifiable Multicast

Let
s
be a value which is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures and let
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. Moreover, let
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathcal{P}

be a designated set. Then protocol

\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}([s],S_{q},\mathcal{R})
allows all the (honest) parties in
\mathcal{R}
to reconstruct the share
[s]_{q}
without disclosing any additional information.
For this, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
reveals

[s]_{q}
to
all
the parties
outside

\mathcal{W}_{q}
, who are in
\mathcal{R}

(the parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
who are in
\mathcal{R}

already
have
[s]_{q}
). To ensure that
P_{i}

does not
cheat,
P_{i}
actually reveals the IC-signature of
every
party in

\mathcal{W}_{q}
on the revealed
[s]_{q}
. The idea here is that since
\mathcal{W}_{q}
has
at least
one
honest
party (irrespective of the network type),
a potentially
corrupt

P_{i}
will fail to reveal the signature of an
honest
party from
\mathcal{W}_{q}
on an
incorrect

[s]_{q}
.
On the other hand, an
honest

P_{i}
will be able to reveal the signature of
all
the parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
on
[s]_{q}
.

Based on
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
, we design another protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}([s],\mathcal{R})
, which allows all the (honest) parties in
\mathcal{R}
to reconstruct
s
.
The idea is to run an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
for
every

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
.
Since the protocols are standard, we present them
and prove there properties (Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
)
in Appendix
0.D
.

Lemma 3

Let
s
be a value which is linearly secret-shared with IC signatures, let
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
be a designated set and let
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be a designated
set of receivers. Then protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
achieves the following.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
: In a synchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
[s]_{q}
after time
T_{\mathsf{RecShare}}=T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,
except with a probability of
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: In an asynchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
eventually output
[s]_{q}
,
except with a probability of
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

Privacy
: If
\mathcal{R}
consists of only honest parties, then the view of the adversary remains independent of
[s]_{q}
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{R}|\cdot n^{3}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|})
bits are communicated.

Lemma 4

Let
s
be a value which is linearly secret-shared with IC signatures and let
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be a set of designated receivers.
Then protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
achieves the following.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
: In a synchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
s
after time
T_{\mathsf{Rec}}=T_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
,
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(|\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}|\cdot n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: In an asynchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
eventually output
s
,
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(|\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}|\cdot n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

Privacy
: If
\mathcal{R}
consists of only honest parties, then the view of the adversary remains independent of
s
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot|\mathcal{R}|\cdot n^{3}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|})
bits are communicated.

6.1
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
and Reconstruction Protocol for Superpolynomial
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|

From Theorem
5.1
and Lemma
4
, the error probability of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
depend linearly on
|\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}|
, which is the same as
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.
This is because there are
\Omega(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}/\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in which the

unforgeability/non-repudiation
properties might get violated with probability
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}
. This might be
problematic for a “large-sized”
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
. To avoid this, we use the idea of
local dispute control

used in
[
40
,
4
]
, which ensures that
irrespective
of the number of instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}/\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, the
overall
error probability is
only

\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
. This is done by ensuring that the
unforgeability/non-repudiation
properties get violated
only

\mathcal{O}(n^{3})
times across all these instances.
The idea here is that the parties start
locally discarding
corrupt parties the “moment”
they are caught cheating during
any
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
or
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.
Once a party
P_{j}
is locally discarded by any party
P_{i}
, then
P_{i}

“behaves” as if
P_{j}
has
certainly
behaved maliciously in all the “future” instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
or
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, even
if this is
not
the case.
This restricts the number of attempts of cheating for the adversary to a “fixed” number and consequently,
the
total
error probability of
arbitrary
many instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}/\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
will
no
longer depend on
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.
To incorporate the above idea, each party
P_{i}
now maintains a list of
locally discarded
parties
\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
, which
it keeps populating across all the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, as soon as
P_{i}
identifies any party
cheating. It will be ensured that an
honest

P_{i}

never
includes an
honest

P_{j}
to
\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
.
We next discuss the modifications in
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
and
how the parties populate their
\mathcal{LD}
sets across instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.

Populating
\mathcal{LD}
Sets During Instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.

In any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
, if
P_{i}
is present in the corresponding set of supporting verifiers
\mathcal{SV}
(i.e.
P_{i}\in\mathcal{SV}
),
then
P_{i}
includes the corresponding
signer

P_{j}
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
instance to
\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
if the following condition holds during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
:

(P_{j}\mbox{ broadcasts }\mathsf{OK})\quad\wedge\quad(B(\alpha_{i})\neq dv_{i}+m_{i}),

where
B(x)
is the masked polynomial broadcasted by the corresponding
\mathsf{I}
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
instance.
The idea here is that if
P_{i}
is
honest
and if the above condition holds, then clearly the signer
P_{j}
is
corrupt
and is trying to break the
non-repudiation

property.
Once
P_{j}\in\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
, then in any pair of
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}},\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}})
instances involving
P_{j}
as the
signer
,
if
P_{i}
is present in the corresponding set
\mathcal{SV}
, then in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
instance,

P_{i}
reveals
\bot
as its verification information to the corresponding
receiver

\mathsf{R}
.
13
13

13

This serves as an indicator for
\mathsf{R}
that
P_{i}
is in conflict with the signer
P_{j}
.

Upon receiving
\bot
as the verification information, the strategy for
\mathsf{R}
is to
always
accepts it
without
doing any verification,

irrespective
of the polynomial
F(x)

revealed as
\mathsf{ICSig}
by the corresponding
\mathsf{I}
.

The above modification ensures that if in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
involving a
corrupt signer

P_{j}
and an
honest

\mathsf{I}
,

P_{j}
distributes an inconsistent verification point
to an
honest verifier

P_{i}
from the corresponding
\mathcal{SV}
set and
still
broadcasts an
\mathsf{OK}
message during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
,
then
P_{j}
will locally be discarded by the verifier
P_{i}
, except with probability
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}
(follows from the
non-repudiation
property of ICP).
From then onwards, in
all
the instances of
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}},\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}})
,
involving
P_{j}
as the
signer
,
if the verifier
P_{i}
is added to the
\mathcal{SV}
set, then the “special”
verification information revealed by
P_{i}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
will
always
be considered as accepted, irrespective of what
verification information
it actually receives from
P_{j}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.
Hence,
P_{j}
will
not
have any chance of cheating the verifier
P_{i}
in any
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
instance. By considering all possibilities
for a
corrupt

\mathsf{S}
and an
honest
verifier
P_{i}
, along with an
honest

\mathsf{I}
, it follows that
except with probability at most
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
the verification-points of
all
honest verifiers from corresponding
\mathcal{SV}
,
will be
accepted
by every
honest

\mathsf{R}
,
during
all
the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
or
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
.
Consequently, except with probability at most
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the signatures revealed by all
honest

\mathsf{I}
will be
always accepted
.

We stress that the above modification
does not
help a
corrupt

\mathsf{I}
to break the

unforgeability
property for an
honest

\mathsf{S}
and an
honest

\mathsf{R}
, with the help
of potentially
corrupt
verifiers.

Populating
\mathcal{LD}
Sets During Instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
.

Consider an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
involving
P_{i}
as
\mathsf{R}
and
P_{j}
as
\mathsf{I}
.
If
P_{i}
finds that
P_{j}
has tried to
forge
signature on an
incorrect
value, then
P_{i}

adds
P_{j}
to
\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
. To achieve this goal, during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,

P_{i}
(as
\mathsf{R}
) now
additionally
checks if there exists a subset of verifiers
\mathcal{SV}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{SV}
, where
\mathcal{SV}\setminus\mathcal{SV}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
, such that
the verification-points of
all
the parties in
\mathcal{SV}^{\prime\prime}
are
rejected
. If such a subset
\mathcal{SV}^{\prime\prime}
exists, then clearly
P_{j}
(as
\mathsf{I}
) has cheated and tried to break the

unforgeability
property, since

\mathcal{SV}^{\prime\prime}
is bound to contain at least one
honest
verifier. If the verification point of an honest verifier is
rejected
, then clearly
P_{j}
is
corrupt
.
Once
P_{j}\in\mathcal{LD}^{(i)}
, from then onwards,
in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
involving
P_{j}
as
\mathsf{I}
and
P_{i}
as
\mathsf{R}
, party

P_{i}

always rejects
any IC-signature revealed by
P_{i}
.

The above modification ensures that if in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
involving an
honest
signer, a
corrupt intermediary

P_{j}
and an
honest receiver

P_{i}
,
P_{j}
tries to reveal an incorrect
signature during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,
then except with probability
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}
, the intermediary
P_{j}
will be locally discarded by the receiver
P_{i}
(follows from the
unforgeability
property of ICP).
From then onwards, in
all
the instances of
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}},\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}})
,
involving
P_{j}
as the intermediary and
P_{i}
as the receiver, the signature revealed by
P_{j}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
will
always
be rejected, irrespective of what
data is actually revealed by
P_{j}
.
Hence, by considering all possibilities for a
corrupt

\mathsf{I}
,

honest

\mathsf{S}
and
honest

\mathsf{R}
,
it follows that except with probability at most
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
no
corrupt

\mathsf{I}
will be able to forge an
honest

\mathsf{S}
’s signature to any
honest

\mathsf{R}
,
in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
, during any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
or
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
.

6.2
Network Agnostic Secure Multicast

Based on protocols
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
, we design a secure verifiable multicast protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
. In the protocol, there exists a designed
sender

\mathsf{Sen}\in\mathcal{P}
with input
v\in\mathbb{F}
and a designated set of

receivers

\mathcal{R}
. The goal is to let every party in
\mathcal{R}
receive
v
, without revealing any additional information to the adversary.
14
14

14

Note that the requirements here are different
from broadcast since we need the
privacy
of
v
if
\mathsf{Sen}
is
honest
and if
\mathcal{R}
consists of
only
honest parties.

While in a
synchronous
network, the (honest) parties in
\mathcal{R}
get
v
after a “fixed” time, in an
asynchronous
network, they do so eventually.
Note that if
\mathsf{Sen}
is
corrupt
, then the parties
need not
obtain any output, as
\mathsf{Sen}

may not
invoke the protocol.
However, if
any
honest party in
\mathcal{R}
computes an output
v^{\star}
(which could be
different
from
v
), then
all
honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
will also output
v^{\star}
.
The “verifiability” here guarantees that in case the honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
get any output, then
all
the (honest) parties in
\mathcal{P}
will be
“aware” of this; namely there will be a Boolean variable
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
, which all the honest parties will set to
1
.

The idea behind
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
is very simple. The parties participate in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, where
\mathsf{Sen}
plays the role of the
dealer
with input
v
.
Once any (honest) party computes an output during
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
(implying that
\mathsf{Sen}
is committed to some value
v^{\star}
which is the same as
v
for an
honest

\mathsf{Sen}
),
then it turns
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1
.
Once
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
is turned to
1
, the parties invoke an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
to let
only
the parties in
\mathcal{R}
reconstruct the committed value.
Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
and proof of its properties (stated in Lemma
5
), are available in Appendix
0.D
.

Lemma 5

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
achieves the following, where
\mathsf{Sen}
participates with input
v
and where each honest party initializes
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
0
.

–

Synchronous Network
: If
\mathsf{Sen}
is honest, then all honest parties set
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1
at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}

and except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
v
, after time

T_{\mathsf{SVM}}=T_{\mathsf{VSS}}+T_{\mathsf{Rec}}
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{R}
consists of only honest parties, then the view of
\mathsf{Adv}
remains independent of
v
.
If
\mathsf{Sen}
is corrupt and some honest party sets
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1
, then there exists some
v^{\star}
such that,
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
v^{\star}
. Moreover, if any honest party sets

\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1
at time
T
,
then all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
v^{\star}
by time
T+2\Delta
.

–

Asynchronous Network
: If
\mathsf{Sen}
is honest, then all honest parties eventually set
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1

and except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
eventually output
v
.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{R}
consists of only honest parties, then the view of the adversary remains independent of
v
.
If
\mathsf{Sen}
is corrupt and some honest party sets
\mathsf{flag}^{(\mathsf{Sen},\mathcal{R})}
to
1
, then there exists some
v^{\star}
such that ,
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
eventually output
v^{\star}
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot n^{8}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits are communicated.

7
Network Agnostic Protocol for Generating Linearly Secret-Shared Random Values with IC-Signatures

In this section, we present a network agnostic protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
, which allows the parties to jointly generate
linear secret-sharing of random values with IC-signatures.
To design the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
, we first design a subprotocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
.

7.1
Network Agnostic VSS for Multiple Dealers

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
(Fig
8
) is a multi-dealer VSS.
In the protocol, each party
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
participates as a dealer with some input
s^{(\ell)}
.
Then,
irrespective
of the network type, the protocol outputs a
common
subset of dealers
\mathsf{CORE}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, which is
guaranteed
to have at least one
honest
dealer.
Moreover, corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there will be some value, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
, which will be the same as
s^{(\ell)}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
,
such that the values

\{{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. While in a
synchronous
network,
\{[{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}]\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}

is generated after a “fixed” time, in an
asynchronous
network,
\{[{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}]\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
is generated eventually.

The high level overview of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
has been already discussed in detail in
Section
1.2.5
.
15
15

15

Actually, the overview was for the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
, but the same idea is also used in the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
.

The idea is to let every dealer
P_{\ell}
to invoke an instance
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
to secret-share its input. However, we need to take special care to ensure that the inputs of all the dealers in
\mathsf{CORE}
are secret-shared with
common

core-sets. For this, each individual dealer in its instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
computes and publishes as many “legitimate” core-sets as possible and the parties run instances of

agreement on common subset

(ACS) to identify whether “sufficiently many” dealers have published the same legitimate core-sets in their respective instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.
Moreover, to ensure that all the underlying IC-signatures satisfy the linearity property, we first need to identify the dealers who distribute shares as part of their respective

\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
instances. For this, we let each dealer distribute shares in its instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
through instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
. This enables the parties to identify
a set of
committed dealers

\mathcal{CD}
who have indeed distributed shares as part of their
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
instances through instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
.

\justify

–

Committing Shares
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
executes the following steps.

–

On having input
s^{(i)}
, randomly choose
s^{(i)}_{1},\dots,s^{(i)}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,
such that
s^{(i)}=s^{(i)}_{1}+\dots+s^{(i)}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.
Act as
\mathsf{Sen}
and invoke instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{i},s^{(i)}_{1},S_{1}),\ldots,\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{i},s^{(i)}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},S_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|})
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
.

–

Corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
, participate in the instances
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
, invoked by
P_{\ell}
as a
\mathsf{Sen}

and
wait till the local time becomes
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}
. For
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
,
let
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
be the Boolean flag, corresponding to the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
, invoked by
P_{\ell}
.

–

Identifying the Set of Committed Dealers Through ACS
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following.

–

For
\ell=1,\ldots,n
,
participate in an instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
1
, provided
P_{i}
has set

\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}=1
, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.

–

Once there exists a subset of dealers
\mathcal{CD}_{i}
where
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that
corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}_{i}
, the instance

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
has produced output
1
, then participate with input
0
in all the BA instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
, for which no input is provided yet.

–

Once all the
n
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
have produced a binary output, set
\mathcal{CD}
to be the set of dealers
P_{\ell}
,
such that

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
has produced output
1
.

–

Exchanging IC-Signed Values
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits till the local time becomes
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Then corresponding to each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
, does the following.

–

For each
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
such that
P_{i}\in S_{q}
,
upon computing an output
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
,
give
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qi})
to every
P_{j}\in S_{q}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
,

where the parties follow the linearity principle while generating IC-signatures
.

–

Announcing Results of Pairwise Consistency Tests
:
Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits till the local time becomes
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}

and then does the following, corresponding to each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
.

–

Upon receiving
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
from
P_{j}
for each
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}
such that
P_{j},P_{i}\in S_{q}
, corresponding to every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
,
broadcast
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
, if

s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{qj}
holds.

–

Corresponding to every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}
, participate in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
initiated by
P_{j}
as a sender, to broadcast any
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(P_{j},\star)
message.

–

Constructing Consistency Graphs
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits till the local time becomes
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}

and then does the following, corresponding to each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
.

–

Construct an undirected consistency graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
with
\mathcal{P}
as the vertex set, where
the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is added to
G^{(\ell,i)}
, provided
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(j,k)
and
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(k,j)
is received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively (through any mode).

–

Public Announcement of Core Sets by the Committed Dealers
: Each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}

waits till its local time is
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, and then
executes the following steps to compute core sets.

–

\forall S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, once

S_{p}
forms a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, compute core-set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}

and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
with respect to
S_{p}
as follows, followed by broadcasting

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
.

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition,
then set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
and include
q
to
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

–

Identifying Valid Core Sets
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits for time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
and then initializes a set
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\emptyset
.
Corresponding to
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}

and
p=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, party
P_{i}
includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, provided
all
the following hold.

–

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

is received from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
, such that for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, the following hold.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then the set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
.

–

If
(q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p})
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, such that:

–

If
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
, then
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
.

–

Else if
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, then

(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

Selecting the Common Committed Dealers and Core Sets through ACS
: Each party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following.

–

For
p=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, participate in an instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
1
, provided there exists a set of dealers

{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}

where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}

and where
(P_{\ell},S_{p})\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
for every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}
.

–

Once any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
has produced an output
1
, participate with input
0
in all the BA instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
, for which no input is provided yet.

–

Once all the
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
have produced a binary output, set
q_{\mathsf{core}}
to be the least index among
\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\}
, such that

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
has produced output
1
.

–

Once
q_{\mathsf{core}}
is computed, then corresponding to each
P_{j}\in\mathcal{CD}
,
participate in an instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
1
, provided
(P_{j},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
.

–

Once there exists a set of parties
{\mathcal{}B}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
,
such that
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
has produced output
1
, corresponding to each
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{i}
,
participate with input
0
in all the instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
, for which no input is provided yet.

–

Once all the
|\mathcal{CD}|

instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
have produced a binary output, include all the parties
P_{j}
from
\mathcal{CD}
in
\mathsf{CORE}
(initialized to
\emptyset
), such that
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
has produced output
1
.

–

Computing Output
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following, after computing
\mathsf{CORE}
and
q_{\mathsf{core}}
.

–

If
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(j)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

is not yet received from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
for for any
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, then wait to receive it
from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
through fallback-mode.

–

Once
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(j)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

is available for every

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, compute
\mathcal{W}_{q}
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
as follows.

–

If
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}=S_{q}
for every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, then set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=S_{q}
.

–

Else set
\mathcal{W}_{q}=(S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
such that
P_{i}\in S_{q}
, compute the output as follows.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, then set
[s^{(\ell)}]_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, where
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
was received from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
,
as part of
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\allowbreak\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(j)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})
.
Moreover, for every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, set

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s^{(\ell)}]_{q})
to the default value.

–

Else, set
[s^{(\ell)}]_{q}
to
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
, where
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
was computed as output during
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
. Moreover,
if
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, then
for every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, set

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s^{(\ell)}]_{q})
to
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
, received from
P_{j}
.

Output
\mathsf{CORE}
, the core sets
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, shares

\{[s^{(\ell)}]_{q}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}\;\wedge\;P_{i}\in S_{q}}
and
the IC-signatures
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s^{(\ell)}]_{q})_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}\;\wedge\;P_{j},P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q},P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

Figure 8
:
The statistically-secure VSS protocol for multiple dealers to generate linearly secret-shared values with
IC-signatures

The properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, stated in Theorem
7.1
are proved in Appendix
0.E
.

Theorem 7.1

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
achieves the following where each
P_{\ell}
participates with input
s^{(\ell)}
and where
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}=T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+6T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Correctness&Commitment
: If the network is synchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
at time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, all honest parties output a common set
\mathsf{CORE}\subseteq\mathcal{P}

such that at least one honest party will be present in
\mathsf{CORE}
.
Moreover, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there exists some
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
, where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}=s^{(\ell)}
for an honest
P_{\ell}
,
such that the values
\{{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Correctness&Commitment
: If the network is asynchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, almost-surely
all honest parties output a common set
\mathsf{CORE}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
eventually
such that at least one honest party will be present in
\mathsf{CORE}
.
Moreover, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there exists some
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
, where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}=s^{(\ell)}
for an honest
P_{\ell}
,
such that the values
\{{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are eventually linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures.

–

Privacy
: Irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
s^{(\ell)}
, corresponding to every honest
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}\cdot n^{9}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits are communicated by
the honest parties. In addition,

\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|+n)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
are invoked.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
with
L
Values for Each Dealer.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
participates with a
single
input.
Consider a scenario where
each

P_{\ell}
participates with
L
inputs
\overrightarrow{S^{(\ell)}}=(s^{(\ell,1)},\ldots,s^{(\ell,L)})
, where
L\geq 1
.
The goal is to identify a
common
subset of dealers
\mathsf{CORE}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
which is
guaranteed
to have at least one
honest
dealer, irrespective of the network type. Corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there exist
L
values, say
\overrightarrow{{S^{\star}}^{(\ell)}}=({s^{\star}}^{(\ell,1)},\ldots,{s^{\star}}^{(\ell,L)})
,
which will be the same as
\overrightarrow{S^{(\ell)}}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
, where all the values in

\{\overrightarrow{{S^{\star}}^{(\ell)}}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. To achieve this, we run the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
with the following modifications,
so that the number of instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the protocol
still
remains to be
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|+n)
, which is
independent
of
L
.

Corresponding to each
s^{(\ell)}\in\overrightarrow{S^{(L)}}
, the dealer
P_{\ell}
will pick
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
random shares (which sum up to
s^{(\ell)}
) and the shares corresponding to the group

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
are communicated through an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
; hence
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot L
instances are invoked by
P_{\ell}
as a
\mathsf{Sen}
.
Then, while identifying the set of committed dealers
\mathcal{CD}
, parties vote
1
for
P_{\ell}
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
provided the underlying
\mathsf{flag}
variable is set to
1
in
all

the
|Z_{s}|\cdot L
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
invoked by
P_{\ell}
. The rest of the steps for identifying
\mathcal{CD}
remains the same. This way, by executing
only

\mathcal{O}(n)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
, we identify
the set
\mathcal{CD}
.

Next, the parties exchange IC-signatures on their supposedly common shares for each group, corresponding to
all
the
L
values shared by each dealer from
\mathcal{CD}
.
However, each
P_{i}
now broadcasts a
single

\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
message, corresponding to each
P_{j}\in S_{q}
, provided
P_{i}
receives IC-signed common share from
P_{j}

on the behalf of
all
the
L
values, shared by
P_{\ell}
. This ensures that, for each
P_{\ell}
, every
P_{i}
constructs a
single
consistency graph. Next, each dealer
P_{\ell}

computes and broadcasts the candidate core-sets and broadcast-sets, as and when they are ready. The parties identify the
\mathsf{CORE}
set by running
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|+n)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
To avoid repetition, we do not present the formal steps of the modified
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
protocol.
The protocol incurs a communication of
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}\cdot L\cdot n^{9}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits,
apart from
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|+n)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

7.2
Protocol for Generating Secret-Shared Random Values

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
(Fig
9
) allows the parties to jointly generate linear secret-sharings of
L
values with IC-signatures, where
L\geq 1
,
which are random for the adversary. For this, the parties invoke an instance of the (modified)
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
where each dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
participates
with a random vector of
L
values.
Let
\mathsf{CORE}
be the set of common dealers identified during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
. Then for
\mathfrak{l}=1,\ldots,L
, the parties output the sum of
{\mathfrak{l}}^{th}

value shared by
all
the dealers in
\mathsf{CORE}
. Since there will be at least one
honest
dealer in
\mathsf{CORE}
whose shared values will be random for the adversary,
it follows that the resultant values also remain random for the adversary.

In the rest of the paper, we will refer to the core sets
\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
obtained during
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
as
global core-sets
and denote them by
\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.
From now onwards, all the secret-shared values will be generated with respect to these global core-sets.

\justify

–

Secret-Sharing Random Values
:
Each
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
picks
L
random values
\overrightarrow{R^{(\ell)}}=(r^{(\ell,1)},\ldots,r^{(\ell,L)})
and participates in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
with input

\overrightarrow{R^{(\ell)}}
and
waits for time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
.

–

Computing Output
: Let
(\mathsf{CORE},\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\{([{r^{\star}}^{(\ell,1)}],\ldots,[{r^{\star}}^{(\ell,L)}])\}_{P_{\ell\in\mathsf{CORE}}})

be the output from the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
. For
\mathfrak{l}=1,\ldots,L
, the parties locally compute

[r^{(\mathfrak{l})}]=\displaystyle\sum_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}[{r^{\star}}^{(\ell,\mathfrak{l})}]
from
\{[{r^{\star}}^{(\ell,\mathfrak{l})}]\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
.
The parties then output
({\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\{[r^{(\mathfrak{l})}]\}_{\mathfrak{l}=1,\ldots,L})
, where
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{q}=\mathcal{W}_{q}
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.

Figure 9
:
Protocol for generating linearly secret-shared random values with
IC-signatures

Theorem
7.2
follows easily from the above discussion.

Theorem 7.2

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
achieves the following where
T_{\mathsf{Rand}}=T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}=T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+6T_{\mathsf{BA}}
and
L\geq 1
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
: If the network is synchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
at the time
T_{\mathsf{Rand}}
, there exist values
r^{(1)},\ldots,r^{(L)}
, which are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, where the core-sets are

\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: If the network is asynchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
there exist values
r^{(1)},\ldots,r^{(L)}
, which are almost-surely linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, where the core-sets are

\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.

–

Privacy
: Irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
r^{(1)},\ldots,r^{(L)}
.

–

Communication Complexity
: The protocol incurs a communication of

\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|^{2}\cdot L\cdot n^{9}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits,
apart from
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|+n)
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

8
Network Agnostic Protocol for Generating Random Multiplication Triples

In this section, we present our network-agnostic triple-generation protocol, which generates random and private multiplication-triples which are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures.
The protocol is based on several sub-protocols which we present next.
Throughout this section, we will assume the existence of
global
core-sets
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, where
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}({\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.
Looking ahead, these core-sets will be generated by first running the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
, using an appropriate value of
L
,
which will be determined across all the sub-protocols which we will be discussing next. All the secret-shared values in the various sub-protocols in the sequel will have
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}

as underlying core-sets.

8.1
Verifiably Generating Linear Secret Sharing of a Value with IC-signatures

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
(Fig
10
), there exists a designated dealer
\mathsf{D}\in\mathcal{P}
with private input
s
. In addition, there is
a random value
r\in\mathbb{F}
, which is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures,
such that the underlying core-sets are
\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
(the value
r
will
not
be known to
\mathsf{D}
at the beginning of the protocol).
The protocol allows the parties to let
\mathsf{D}
verifiably generate a linear secret-sharing of
s
with
IC-signatures, such that the underlying core-sets are
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, where
s
remains private for an
honest

\mathsf{D}
.
The verifiability guarantees that even if
\mathsf{D}
is
corrupt
, if any (honest) party computes an output, then there exists
some
value, say
s^{\star}
, which is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures,
such that the underlying core-sets are
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.

The protocol idea is very simple and standard. We first let
\mathsf{D}
reconstruct the value
r
, which is then used as a
one-time pad
(OTP) by
\mathsf{D}
to make public an OTP-encryption of
s
.
Then, using the linearity property of secret-sharing, the parties locally remove the OTP from the OTP-encryption.

\justify

–

Reconstructing the OTP Towards the Dealer
: The parties in
\mathcal{P}
invoke an instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}([r],\{\mathsf{D}\})
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
to let
\mathsf{D}
reconstruct
r

and
wait for time
T_{\mathsf{Rec}}
.

–

Making the OTP-encryption Public
:
\mathsf{D}
, upon computing the output
r
from the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
, broadcasts
\mathbf{s}=s+r
.

–

Computing the Output
: The parties in
\mathcal{P}

wait till the local time becomes

T_{\mathsf{Rec}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Then upon receiving
\mathbf{s}
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, the parties in
\mathcal{P}

locally compute
[\mathbf{s}-r]
from
[\mathbf{s}]
and
[r]
.
Here
[\mathbf{s}]
denotes the
default linear secret-sharing
of
\mathbf{s}
with IC-signatures and
core-sets
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, where
[\mathbf{s}]_{1}=\mathbf{s}
and

[\mathbf{s}]_{2}=\ldots=[\mathbf{s}]_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}=0
, and where the parties set

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[\mathbf{s}]_{q})_{P_{j},P_{i}\in{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{q},P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}}
to the default value
.
The parties then output
({\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}},[\mathbf{s}-r])
.

Figure 10
:
VSS for verifiably generating a linear secret-sharing of a value with
IC-signatures with respect to given global core-sets

The properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
stated in Lemma
6
are proved in Appendix
0.F
.

Lemma 6

Let
r
be a random value which is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures with
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets.
Then protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
achieves the following where
\mathsf{D}
participates with the input
s
.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is honest, then the following hold, where
T_{\mathsf{LSh}}=T_{\mathsf{Rec}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Correctness
: If the network is synchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the honest parties output
[s]
at the time
T_{\mathsf{LSh}}
, with

{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Correctness
: If the network is asynchronous, then except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the honest parties eventually
output
[s]
, with

{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets.

–

Privacy
: Irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
s
.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt then either no honest party computes any output or there exists some value, say
s^{\star}
, such that the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-Commitment
: If the network is synchronous, then
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the honest parties output
[s^{\star}]
, with

{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets. Moreover, if any honest party computes its output at the time
T
, then all honest parties will have their respective output by
the time
T+\Delta
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-Commitment
: If the network is asynchronous, then
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the honest parties eventually output
[s^{\star}]
, with

{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot n^{3}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}+n^{4}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits are communicated by the honest parties.

We end this section with some notations which we use while invoking the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
in the rest of the paper.

Notation 8.1
(Notations for Using Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
)

Let
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
. In the rest of the paper we will say that “
P_{i}
invokes an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
with input
s
” to mean that
P_{i}
acts as
\mathsf{D}
and invokes an instance

\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}(\mathsf{D},s,\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a},\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}},[r],{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|})
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
. Here,
r
will be the corresponding random “pad” for this instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
, which will already be linearly secret-shared
with IC-signatures, with
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets. If there are multiple instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
invoked by
\mathsf{D}
, then corresponding to each instance, there will be a random secret-shared
pad available to the parties beforehand. The parties will be knowing which secret-shared pad is associated with which instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
. This will be ensured by upper-bounding the maximum number of

\Pi_{\mathsf{LSh}}
instances
\mathsf{L_{max}}
invoked across all our protocols. The parties then generate
\mathsf{L_{max}}
number of linearly secret-shared random values with IC-signatures,
with
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets, by running the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rand}}
beforehand with
L=\mathsf{L_{max}}
.

8.2
Non-Robust Multiplication Protocol

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{BasicMult}}
(Fig
11
) takes input
a
and
b
, which are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures, with
{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets
and a
publicly known
subset
\mathcal{GD}\subset\mathcal{P}
, consisting of
only corrupt
parties. The parties output a linear secret-sharing of
c
with IC-signatures, with

{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{1},\ldots,{\bf{\mathcal{GW}}}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
being the underlying core-sets. If all the parties in

\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{GD}
behave
honestly
, then
c=a\cdot b
