T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: In an asynchronous network, the honest parties eventually output
[s]
.

–

Privacy
: Adversary’s view remains independent of
s
in any network.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt, then the following hold.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-commitment
: In a synchronous network, either no honest party obtains any output or there exists some

s^{\star}\in\mathbb{F}
, such that the parties output
[s^{\star}]
.
Moreover, if any honest party computes its output corresponding to
[s^{\star}]
at time
T
, then all honest parties
compute their output corresponding to
[s^{\star}]
by time
T+\Delta
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-commitment
: In an asynchronous network, either no honest party obtains any output or there exists some

s^{\star}\in\mathbb{F}
, such that the honest parties eventually output
[s^{\star}]
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\cdot n^{8}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|}\cdot|\sigma|)
bits are communicated by the honest parties.

This section presents our network-agnostic reconstruction protocols and secure multicast protocol, along with their
properties. We start with the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}

for reconstructing a designated share,
presented in Fig
18
.

\justify

–

Sending IC-signed Share to the Parties
: If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, then
reveal
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[s]_{q})
of every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
to every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
. Here
\mathcal{W}_{q}
denotes the publicly known core-set corresponding to
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, as part of

[s]
.

–

Computing Output
: If
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{W}_{q})
, then output
[s]_{q}
.
Else if
P_{i}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
, then
check
if there exists any
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and a value
s_{qj}
, such that
P_{i}
has accepted

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},s_{qj})
of every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
. Upon finding such a
P_{j}
,
output
[s]_{q}=s_{qj}
.
20
20

20

If there are multiple such parties
P_{j}

Figure 18
:
Network agnostic
reconstruction protocol to reconstruct a designated share
[s]_{q}

.

We next prove the properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
.

Lemma
3
.

Let
s
be a value which is linearly secret-shared with IC signatures, let
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
be a designated set and let
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be a designated
set of receivers. Then protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{RecShare}}
achieves the following.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
: In a synchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
output
[s]_{q}
at time
T_{\mathsf{RecShare}}=T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
,
except with a probability of
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
: In an asynchronous network, all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
eventually output
[s]_{q}
,
except with a probability of
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

Privacy
: If
\mathcal{R}
consists of only honest parties, then the view of the adversary remains independent of
[s]_{q}
.

–

Communication Complexity
:
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{R}|\cdot n^{3}\cdot\log{|\mathbb{F}|})
bits are communicated.

Proof

We first note that all honest parties in
(\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{W}_{q})
output
[s]_{q}
correctly. So consider an
arbitrary
honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
.
We first show that
P_{i}
indeed computes an output in the protocol, irrespective of the network type.

Since
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies
the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition, it contains
at least
one
honest
party, say

P_{j}
. Since
P_{j}
follows the protocol steps honestly, it reveals
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},[s]_{q})
of
every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
to
P_{i}
.
From the
correctness
properties of ICP (see Theorem
4.1
),
it follows that
P_{i}
will accept the IC-signatures

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},[s]_{q})
, revealed by
P_{j}
, after time

T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in a
synchronous
network, or eventually in an
asynchronous
network.
On the other hand, even if
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
is
corrupt
, then also
from the
non-repudiation
properties of ICP, it follows
that
P_{i}

accepts
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},[s]_{q})
, except with a probability
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}
,
after time

T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in a
synchronous
network, or eventually in an
asynchronous
network.
As there can be
\mathcal{O}(n)

corrupt
parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
, from the union bound, it follows that
except with a probability
\mathcal{O}(n\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, party
P_{i}
will find a candidate party from
\mathcal{W}_{q}
, who reveals
[s]_{q}
, along with the IC-signature of all the parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
,
after time

T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in a
synchronous
network, or eventually in an
asynchronous
network.
This is because the
honest
party in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
always constitutes a candidate party.
Now as there can be
\mathcal{O}(n)
parties in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
, it follows that
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, every honest party in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
will
find a candidate party from
\mathcal{W}_{q}
, who reveals
[s]_{q}
, along with the IC-signature of all the parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
,
after time

T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in a
synchronous
network, or eventually in an
asynchronous
network.
Hence all honest parties in
\mathcal{R}
compute an output, after time

T_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
in a
synchronous
network, or eventually in an
asynchronous
network, except
with a probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

We next show that the output computed by all the honest parties is indeed correct. While this is trivially true for the parties in
(\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{W}_{q})
,
consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
.
The above argument shows that
P_{i}
computes an output in the protocol, irrespective of the network type. So let
P_{i}
output
s^{\prime}
.
We wish to show that
s^{\prime}=[s]_{q}
. From the protocol steps, since
P_{i}
outputs
s^{\prime}
, it implies that there exists some
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, such that
P_{i}
has accepted

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},s^{\prime})
of every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, revealed by
P_{j}
. If
P_{j}
is
honest
, then indeed
s^{\prime}=[s]_{q}
, as one of the IC-signatures

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},s^{\prime})
is the same as
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},[s]_{q})
, corresponding to the
honest

P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
, which is guaranteed to exist.
So consider the case when
P_{j}
is
corrupt
.
Moreover, let
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
be an
honest
party (which is guaranteed to exist).
In order that
s^{\prime}\neq[s]_{q}
, it must be the case that
P_{i}
accepts
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{k},P_{j},P_{i},s^{\prime})
, revealed by
P_{j}
. However, from
the
unforgeability
property of ICP (see Theorem
4.1
), this can happen only with probability
\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}}
.
Now as there can be up to
\mathcal{O}(n)
corrupt parties in
\mathcal{W}_{q}
, from the union bound, it follows that the probability that
P_{i}
outputs

s^{\prime}\neq[s]_{q}
is at most
\mathcal{O}(n\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.
And since there can be up to
\mathcal{O}(n)
parties in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}
, it follows that except with probability at most

\mathcal{O}(n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the output of every honest party in
\mathcal{R}
is indeed
[s]_{q}
.

Communication complexity follows from the communication complexity of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
(Theorem
4.1
) and the fact that
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{R}|\cdot n^{2})
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Reveal}}
are involved.
And privacy follows from the privacy of ICP.

P_{i}\in\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle s=\sum_{S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}}[s]_{q}
.

Appendix 0.E
Properties of the Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}

In this section, we prove the properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
(see Fig
8
for the formal description).

We first show that if the network is
synchronous
, then all honest parties will compute a
common
candidate set of committed dealers
\mathcal{CD}
set by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, such that
all
honest dealers
are guaranteed to be present in
\mathcal{CD}
.

Lemma 49

If the network is synchronous and
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
is an honest dealer participating with input
s^{(\ell)}
, then all the following hold in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, where
\mathcal{H}
is the set of
honest parties.

–

Except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a common
\mathcal{CD}
set by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, where

\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
.
21
21

21

This automatically implies that
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
.

–

Except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,
every party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, for an honest
P_{\ell}
.

Proof

Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of
honest
parties.
From the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
in the
synchronous
network (Lemma
5
),
it follows that every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will set
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
to
1
at time

, corresponding to every

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. Moreover, at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}
, corresponding to every

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, each
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
computes the output
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
.
Furthermore, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the value
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
will be the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Hence, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will start participating with input
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
, at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}
.
Hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
), at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
,
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will obtain the output
1
from the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
instances, corresponding to each
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
.
Since
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{H}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, it follows that
at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
start participating with input
0
in any remaining instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
, for which no input is provided yet.
Hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
), all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will compute some output in all the
n

instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. Moreover, the outputs will be common for the parties in
\mathcal{H}
.
Consequently, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a common
\mathcal{CD}
set at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
, since
\mathcal{CD}
includes all the dealers
P_{\ell}
such that
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
outputs
1
. And as shown above
the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
instances corresponding to
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
outputs
1
.

Next, consider an
arbitrary

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
. This implies that at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, at least one party from
\mathcal{H}
, say
P_{k}
,
has participated with input
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
. If not, then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would have obtained the output
0
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
and hence
P_{\ell}\not\in\mathcal{CD}
, which is a

contradiction
. This implies that by the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, party
P_{k}
has set
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
to
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
,
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.
So consider an
arbitrary

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. From the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
in the
synchronous
network (Lemma
5
),
it follows that there exists some value
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
,
such that except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
output
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}+2\Delta<T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

We next show that if the network is
synchronous
and if an
honest
dealer from
\mathcal{CD}

broadcasts any set of candidate core-sets, then all honest parties will “accept” the core-sets.
Moreover, the dealer will
never
make public the share corresponding to the group from
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
consisting of only honest parties while making public these core-sets.
Furthermore, each honest dealer in
\mathcal{CD}
will start making
public
at least
one candidate set of core-sets, namely the one computed with respect to the group from
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
,
consisting of only honest parties. A consequence of all these properties is that if the dealer is honest, the adversary will not learn any information about the dealer’s input.

Lemma 50

If the network is synchronous and
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
is an honest dealer participating with input
s^{(\ell)}
, then all the following hold in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
except with
probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, where
\mathcal{H}
is the set of honest parties.

–

If
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
, then
P_{\ell}
will broadcast

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\allowbreak\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
at time

T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

If
P_{\ell}
broadcasts any
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
at time

T
, then every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
will include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Moreover, the following will hold.

–

If
S_{q}=\mathcal{H}
, then
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

–

For
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, each
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will be either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
. Moreover,

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
will satisfy the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

If
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then every honest
P_{i}\in S_{q}
will have the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Moreover, every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Furthermore, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
of any honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. Also, all the underlying IC-signatures will satisfy the linearity property.

–

The view of the adversary will be independent of
s^{(\ell)}
.

Proof

Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of
honest
parties.
Since the dealer
P_{\ell}
is
honest
, from Lemma
49
it follows that corresponding to
every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, every party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will have the share
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
, by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, where

s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Now consider an
arbitrary

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
.
At time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, each party
P_{i}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
starts giving

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qi})
to every
P_{j}\in S_{q}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, where
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds.
Then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
4.1
),
it follows that at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}
, each party
P_{i}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
will receive

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
from every
P_{j}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, such that
s^{(\ell)}_{qj}=s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds.
Since
S_{q}
is arbitrary, it follows that at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}
, every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
broadcasts an
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
message, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
. From the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.2
), it follows that
these
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
messages are received by every party in
\mathcal{H}
through
regular-mode
at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Consequently, the set
\mathcal{H}
constitutes a clique in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
of every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Note that the set
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. Let
S_{p}
be the set from
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, such that
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
.
From the protocol steps, it then follows that at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, the dealer
P_{\ell}
will compute core-sets
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}

for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
with respect to
S_{p}
as follows.

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition,
then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set to
S_{q}
and
q
is included to
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

After computing the core-sets and broadcast-set,
P_{\ell}
will broadcast

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
at time

T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. This proves the first part of the lemma.

We next proceed to prove the second part of the lemma. So consider an
arbitrary

S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, such that
P_{\ell}
compute core-sets
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}

for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
with respect to
S_{p}
and broadcasts

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
at time
T
.
This means at time
T
, the parties in
S_{p}
constitute a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
.
We also note that
T\geq T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. This is because any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
takes at least
T_{\mathsf{BC}}
time in a
synchronous
network to generate an output.
And the parties in
\mathcal{H}
start participating in any
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
instance invoked for broadcasting any
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)
message, only
after
time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}
.
Consequently,
any
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)
message received by
P_{\ell}
, must be
after
time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
We also note that any edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
which is present in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
of
P_{\ell}
at time
T
, is bound to be present in the graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
of every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
, latest
by time
T+\Delta
. This is because the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is added to
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
upon the receipt of
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(j,k)
and
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(k,j)
messages from the broadcast of
P_{j}

and
P_{k}
respectively. And from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity,
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-consistency
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, these
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)

messages will be received by every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
, latest by time
T+\Delta
.
Since
P_{\ell}
is assumed to be
honest
, it follows that the sets
\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
and
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
satisfy the following properties.

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition,
then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set to
S_{q}
and
q
is included to
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

We also note that if
S_{q}=\mathcal{H}
, then
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
and consequently,
P_{\ell}
will
not
make the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
public.
This is because
T\geq T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. And as shown in the proof of the first part, the set
\mathcal{H}
will constitute a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
at time

T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Since
P_{\ell}
is
honest
, from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, it follows that all the parties in

\mathcal{H}
will receive
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

through the regular-mode at time
T+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Moreover, each party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to the set
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
This is because since
P_{\ell}
has computed the sets

\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
and
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}

honestly
, these sets will pass all the verifications for each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
at time
T+\Delta
.

Next consider an
arbitrary

q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
. This implies that
P_{\ell}
has set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
because
the parties in
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
. Now consider an
arbitrary

P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
.
This implies that
P_{i}
has computed
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})

at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}
, which will be the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, since
P_{\ell}
is
honest
.
Next consider
arbitrary

P_{i},P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
, such that
P_{j}\neq P_{i}
. This implies that the edge
(i,j)
is present in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, which further implies that

P_{i}
has broadcasted the message
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
. This further implies that
P_{i}
must have received

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
from
P_{j}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, such that
s^{(\ell)}_{qj}=s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
holds.
Since
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, it follows that
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
is the same as
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{q})
.
On the other hand, consider an
arbitrary

P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
, such that
P_{j}
is
corrupt
and where
P_{j}
has received

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
from
P_{i}
, for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Then from the protocol steps, it follows that
s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
, since
P_{i}
gives the IC-signature on the share
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
, received from
P_{\ell}
.
And since
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, it follows that
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
is the same as
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{q})
.
The linearity of the underlying IC-signatures follow from the fact the parties follow the linearity principle while generating IC-signatures.

Finally, the privacy of
s^{(\ell)}
follows from the fact that throughout the protocol, adversary
does not
learn anything about the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, provided
S_{q}=\mathcal{H}
.
Namely, during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
where

(S_{q}\cap Z^{\star})=\emptyset
, the view of the adversary remains independent of
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which follows from the privacy of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
(Lemma
5
).
Moreover, as shown above,
P_{\ell}
never makes public the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, as
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.
Furthermore, since the set
\mathcal{H}
will consists of
only
honest parties, from the
privacy
of ICP (see Theorem
4.1
), it follows that the adversary does not learn any additional information about

s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, when the parties in
\mathcal{H}
exchange IC-signed
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
during the pairwise consistency tests.

We next show that if the network is
synchronous
, then any candidate set of core-sets “accepted” on the behalf of a
corrupt
dealer
by any
honest
party at the time
T
, is bound to be accepted by all honest parties, latest by time
T+\Delta
.

Lemma 51

If the network is synchronous and if in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
any honest party
P_{i}
receives

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of any
corrupt dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T
, then all honest parties
P_{j}
will receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}

and include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{j}
by time
T+\Delta
.
Moreover, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, the following holds, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the

\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

If
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then every honest
P_{i}\in S_{q}
will have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Moreover, every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
and for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Furthermore, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
has
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
of any honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}={s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. Also, all the underlying IC-signatures will satisfy the linearity principle.

Proof

The proof follows very closely the proof of Lemma
50
. Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}

be the set of
honest
parties. Now consider
an
arbitrary corrupt
dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and an
arbitrary

P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
, such that

P_{i}
receives

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of

P_{\ell}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T
. Now consider another
arbitrary

P_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
, such that
P_{j}\neq P_{i}
.
From the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-consistency
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, it follows that
P_{j}
is bound to receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
, latest by time
T+\Delta
. We wish to show that
P_{j}
will include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{j}
, by time
T+\Delta
.
For this, we note that since
P_{i}
has included
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T
,

all
the following conditions hold for
P_{i}
at time
T
,
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then the set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
.

–

If
(q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p})
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, such that:

–

If
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
, then
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
.

–

Else if
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, then

(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

We claim that all the above conditions will hold
even
for
P_{j}
by time
T+\Delta
. This is because
all
the edges which are present in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,i)}

at time
T
are bound to be present in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,j)}
by time
T+\Delta
.
This follows from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity,
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-consistency
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network (see Theorem
3.2
)
and the fact that the edges in the graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
are based on
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)
messages, which are received through various
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
instances.

Next consider an
arbitrary

q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
. This implies that
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
and
all the parties in
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitute a clique in the consistency graph of every party in
\mathcal{H}
.
From the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
in the
synchronous
network, all honest parties in
S_{q}
compute a common output, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, during the instance

\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
.
Next consider an
arbitrary

P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q})
and any
arbitrary

P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
.
This implies that
P_{i}
has broadcasted the message
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
, after receiving

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
from
P_{j}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
,
and verifying that the share
s^{(\ell)}_{qj}
is the
same
as the one, computed during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}^{(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})}
.
Since the share computed by
P_{i}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
is
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, it follows that

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
is the same as
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q})
.
On the other hand, since
P_{i}
gives its IC-signature on
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
to
every

P_{j}\in S_{q}
, it follows that
if any
corrupt

P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
has
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
from
P_{i}
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}={s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. The linearity of the underlying IC-signatures follow from the fact that the parties follow the linearity principle while generating the IC-signatures.

Now based on the previous two lemmas, we show that in a
synchronous
network, all honest parties will output a “legitimate”
set of parties
\mathsf{CORE}
after time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+6T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, such that
at least
one honest party is present in
\mathsf{CORE}
.
And corresponding to every party in
\mathsf{CORE}
, there exists some value, which is linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. Moreover, the values corresponding to the honest parties remain private.

Lemma 52

If the network is synchronous, then in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
at the time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}=T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+6T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all honest parties output a common set
\mathsf{CORE}
,
such that at least one honest party will be present in
\mathsf{CORE}
.
Moreover, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there exists some
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
, where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}=s^{(\ell)}
for an honest
P_{\ell}
,
which is the input of
P_{\ell}
for
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
,
such that the values
\{{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. Furthermore,
if
P_{\ell}
is honest, then adversary’s view is independent of
s^{(\ell)}
.

Proof

Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of honest parties.
From Lemma
49
, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a common set of committed dealers

\mathcal{CD}
by the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, where

\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
. Moreover, corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,
every party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, for an
P_{\ell}
.
We begin by showing that once the set of committed dealers
\mathcal{CD}
is decided, then all the
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}

and then all the
|\mathcal{CD}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
will produce some output, for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
, by time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
.

Consider the set
S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, such that
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
.
Then corresponding to each
P_{\ell}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap\mathcal{CD})
, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
(see Lemma
50
).
Since
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{H}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
and
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
,
it follows that at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will have a set
{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}
(namely
{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}=\mathcal{H}
),
where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}

and where
(P_{\ell},S_{p})\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
for every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}
.
Consequently, each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
starts participating in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}

with input
1
, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+2T_{\mathsf{BA}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
From the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (see Theorem
3.1
),
it follows that at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
obtains the output
1
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
.
Consequently, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
,
every party in
\mathcal{H}
will start participating in the remaining
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instances for which no input has been provided yet (if there are any), with input
0
.
And from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,
these
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instances will produce common outputs, for every party in
\mathcal{H}
, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
As a result, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will compute a
q_{\mathsf{core}}
. Moreover,
q_{\mathsf{core}}
will be
common
for all the
parties in
\mathcal{H}
, since it corresponds to the least-indexed
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instance among
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,1)},\ldots,\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|)}
, which produces output
1
. And from the

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network, each
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q)}
instance produces a common output for every party in
\mathcal{H}
.
We also note that
q_{\mathsf{core}}
will be indeed set to some value from the set
\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\}
. This is because as shown above, the index
p
where
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
always constitute a candidate

q_{\mathsf{core}}
.

We next claim that corresponding to
S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
,
there exists a subset of parties
{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that corresponding to every
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
,
the ordered pair
(P_{j},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
is present in the set
\mathcal{C}_{i}
of every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Assuming that the claim is true, it implies that all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will participate with input
1
in the instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. And hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
all the parties will obtain the output
1
in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. As a result, at time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will start participating
in the remaining
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
instances for which no input has been provided yet (if there are any), with input
0
.
Consequently, from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-security
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
at the time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have some output from all the
|\mathcal{CD}|
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
.
Moreover, the outputs will be
common
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
. Furthermore, the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a subset
\mathsf{CORE}
, which corresponds to all the

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances, which have produced output
1
. Note that
(\mathcal{H}\cap\mathsf{CORE})\neq\emptyset
. This is because
{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\subseteq\mathsf{CORE}
and
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
.
Consequently,
(\mathcal{H}\cap{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})\neq\emptyset
, as otherwise
\mathcal{Z}_{s}

does not
satisfy the
\mathbb{Q}^{(2)}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition, which is a contradiction.

We next proceed to prove our claim. Since the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
has produced output
1
, it follows that at least one party from
\mathcal{H}
, say
P_{k}
, have participated with input
1

in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
. This is because if
all
the parties in
\mathcal{H}
participates with input
0
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
, then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the

synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
), all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would have obtained the output
0
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
, which is a contradiction.
We also note that
P_{k}
would have started participating with input
1
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
, latest by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
This is because as argued above, by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would have started participating in
all
the
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
instances
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
, with
some
input. Now since
P_{k}
has participated with input
1

in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
, it follows that at the time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, there exists a subset of parties
{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
, where

\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that
(P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
is present in the set
\mathcal{C}_{k}
, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
.
We show that the set
{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
constitutes the candidate
{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
. For this, note that for any
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
,
party
P_{k}
includes
(P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, only after receiving
a message
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}

and verifying it. Moreover,
P_{k}
must have received the message

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})
from
each
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
, latest by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
It then follows from Lemma
52
that by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+3T_{\mathsf{BA}}+\Delta<T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
,

every
party in
\mathcal{H}
would have received

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})
from
each
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
. And hence each party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
would include
(P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
to the set
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},k}
,
by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+4T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. This proves our claim.

We next claim that at the time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
would have received a message

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})
from
the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
. The proof for this is very similar to the proof of the previous claim and relies on the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
. So consider an
arbitrary

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
.
This implies that the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\ell)}
has produced output
1
for
all
the parties in
\mathcal{H}
, which further implies that at least one party from
\mathcal{H}
, say
P_{m}
, has participated with input

1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\ell)}
. If not, then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
), the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\ell)}

would have produced output
0
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
, which is a contradiction.
We also note that
P_{m}
must have started participating in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\ell)}
, latest by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
This is because as shown above, by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would have started participating in
all
the
|\mathcal{CD}|

instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
, with some input. Now since
P_{m}
participates with input
1
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\ell)}
, it follows that by time

T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}
, party
P_{m}
must have received a message

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
and included
(P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
to
\mathcal{C}_{m}
.
It then follows from Lemma
52
that by time
T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+5T_{\mathsf{BA}}+\Delta<T_{\mathsf{SVM}}+T_{\mathsf{Auth}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+6T_{\mathsf{BA}}
,

every
party
P_{i}
in
\mathcal{H}
would have received

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})
from

P_{\ell}
and would include
(P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.

Till now we have shown that all the all the
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}

and then all the
|\mathcal{CD}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
will produce some output, for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
, by time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
. Moreover, at the time

T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a common
q_{\mathsf{core}}\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\}
and a common set
\mathsf{CORE}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathsf{CORE}
has at least one
honest
party.
Furthermore, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
would have received a message

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
. Furthermore, from Lemma
52
, corresponding to each
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, the set

\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}
will be either the set
S_{q}
or
(S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\cap S_{q})
, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
. If

\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}=S_{q}
for
every

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, then all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will set
\mathcal{W}_{q}
to
S_{q}
.
On the other hand, if
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}=(S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\cap S_{q})
for
any

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, then all the parties in

\mathcal{H}
will set
\mathcal{W}_{q}
to
(S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\cap S_{q})
. Irrespective of the case, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would set
\mathcal{W}_{q}
to a common subset.
We also note that irrespective of the case,
S_{q}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{q}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
holds. This is because from Lemma
52

and Lemma
51
, the condition
S_{q}\setminus\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
holds, corresponding to
every

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
.

Finally consider an
arbitrary

P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
and an
arbitrary

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. We claim that at the time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, all the parties in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})

will have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
. For this, we consider two possible cases.
If
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, then each
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
would have received
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
, as part of the

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}},q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}})

message. Consequently, in this case
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, received from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
.
On the other hand, if
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, then from Lemma
51
,
each
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
would have a common share, say
{s^{(\star)}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
; moreover, from Lemma
50
,
if
P_{\ell}
is
honest
, then
{s^{(\star)}}^{(\ell)}_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. We define

{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\displaystyle\sum_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}{s^{(\star)}}^{(\ell)}_{q},

where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}=s^{(\ell)}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
.
Hence at time
T_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, each party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
has
[{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}]_{q}
.
We also note that if
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, then
every

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
sets
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},[{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}]_{q})

to the default value, corresponding to every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
On the other hand, if
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
,
then every
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
and for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Furthermore, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{q}
has
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
of any
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}={s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds.
Moreover, from Lemma
51
, if
P_{\ell}
is
honest
, then
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
in the IC-signatures mentioned above will be the same as

s^{(\ell)}_{q}
. It then follows that
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
will be linearly secret-shared; the linearity of the underlying IC-signatures
follows since the (honest) parties follow the linearity principle, while generating the IC-signatures.

The privacy of
s^{(\ell)}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
follows from Lemma
51
.

We next consider an
asynchronous
network. We first prove an analogue of Lemma
49
in the asynchronous network.

Lemma 53

If the network is asynchronous and
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}
is an honest dealer participating with input
s^{(\ell)}
, then all the following hold in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, where
\mathcal{H}
is the set of
honest parties.

–

Except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, almost-surely, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually have a common
\mathcal{CD}
set, where

\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
.

–

Except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,
every party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will eventually have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, for an honest
P_{\ell}
.

Proof

Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of
honest
parties. Note that
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, since
\mathcal{Z}_{a}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{s}
.
From the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Lemma
5
),
it follows that every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually set
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
to
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
, corresponding to every

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. Moreover, corresponding to every

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, each
P_{i}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
eventually computes an output
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}((P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
.
Furthermore, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, the value
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
will be the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
.

We first claim that there always exists a subset of parties
{\mathcal{}D}
, where
\mathcal{P}\setminus{\mathcal{}D}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
eventually produces output
1
for all the
parties in
\mathcal{H}
, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}D}
. Assuming that the claim is true, it implies that all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually participate with some input in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
instances

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1)},\ldots,\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(n)}
. This is because from the protocol steps, once the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
instances corresponding to the parties in
{\mathcal{}D}
produce output
1
, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will start participating with input

0
in the remaining instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
(if any), for which no input has been provided yet.
And hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, it follows that almost-surely, all these
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
instances will eventually produce some output
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
. Moreover, the outputs will be the same for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
. Consequently, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually obtain a common
\mathcal{CD}
set. Moreover,

\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, since
\mathcal{CD}
consists of all those parties
P_{\ell}
, such that the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
produces output
1
. And according to our claim,
{\mathcal{}D}\subseteq\mathcal{CD}
holds. We now
proceed to prove our claim.

There are two possible cases. Consider the case when
some

P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
starts participating with input
0
in any
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
instance. This implies that for
P_{i}
, there exists a subset of parties

\mathcal{CD}_{i}
where
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}_{i}
, the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
has produced output
1
for
P_{i}
. In this case, the set
\mathcal{CD}_{i}
is the
candidate
{\mathcal{}D}
set, whose existence we want to prove. Next, consider the case when
no
party in
\mathcal{H}
has started participating with input
0
in
any
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\star)}
instances.
In this case, the set
\mathcal{H}
constitutes the candidate
{\mathcal{}D}
set. This is because as shown above, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually set
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
to
1
, corresponding to every

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. And hence every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually start participating with input
1
in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
instances, corresponding to
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
.
Consequently, the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
) will guarantee that the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
instances, corresponding to
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
eventually
produce output
1
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
.

Next, consider an
arbitrary

P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
. This implies that at least one party from
\mathcal{H}
, say
P_{k}
,
has participated with input
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
. If not, then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
would have obtained the output
0
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(\ell)}
and hence
P_{\ell}\not\in\mathcal{CD}
, which is a

contradiction
. This implies that party
P_{k}
has set
\mathsf{flag}^{(P_{\ell},S_{q})}
to
1
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
,
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.
So consider an
arbitrary

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
. From the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Lemma
5
),
it follows that there exists some value
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
for an
honest

P_{\ell}
,
such that except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
eventually output
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
.

We next prove the analogue of Lemma
50
in the
asynchronous
network.

Lemma 54

If the network is asynchronous and
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
is an honest dealer participating with input
s^{(\ell)}
, then all the following hold in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
where
\mathcal{H}
is the set of honest parties.

–

If
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
, then
P_{\ell}
will eventually broadcast

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\allowbreak\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
.

–

If
P_{\ell}
broadcasts any
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

then every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
will eventually include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.
Moreover, the following will hold.

–

If
S_{q}=\mathcal{H}
, then
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

–

For
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, each
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will be either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
such that

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

If
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then every honest
P_{i}\in S_{q}
will have the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Moreover, every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Furthermore, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
of any honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. Also, all the underlying IC-signatures will satisfy the linearity property.

–

The view of the adversary will be independent of
s^{(\ell)}
.

Proof

Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of
honest
parties. We first note that
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
,
since
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
as
\mathcal{Z}_{a}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{s}
. The proof for the first part of the lemma is similar to the proof of the first part of Lemma
50
, except that
all the “favourable” conditions hold for an
honest

P_{\ell}

eventually
. In more detail, consider an
arbitrary

S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
.
Then each
P_{i}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
eventually computes the share
s^{(\ell)}_{qi}
during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{SVM}}(P_{\ell},s^{(\ell)}_{q},S_{q})
, where

s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.
Consequently,
P_{i}

starts giving

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qi})
to every
P_{j}\in S_{q}
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Then from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Auth}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
4.1
),
it follows that each party
P_{i}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
will eventually receive

\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},s^{(\ell)}_{qj})
from every
P_{j}\in(S_{q}\cap\mathcal{H})
, for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, such that
s^{(\ell)}_{qj}=s^{(\ell)}_{qi}=s^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds.
Since
S_{q}
is arbitrary, it follows that eventually, every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
broadcasts an
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
message, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
. From the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-weak validity
and
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
3.2
), it follows that
these
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(i,j)
messages are eventually received by every party in
\mathcal{H}
.
Consequently, the set
\mathcal{H}
eventually becomes a clique in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
of every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
.
Let
S_{p}
be the set from
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, such that
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
.
From the protocol steps, it then follows that the dealer
P_{\ell}
will eventually compute core-sets
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}

for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
with respect to
S_{p}
as follows,

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition,
then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set to
S_{q}
and
q
is included to
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

After computing the core-sets and broadcast-set,
P_{\ell}
will eventually broadcast

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
.

We next proceed to prove the second part of the lemma, whose proof is again similar to the proof of the second part of the Lemma
50
, except that all the
“favourable” conditions which hold for
P_{\ell}
, are guaranteed to hold eventually for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
. In more detail,
consider an
arbitrary

S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, such that
P_{\ell}
compute core-sets
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}

for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
and broadcast-set
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
with respect to
S_{p}
and broadcasts

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
.
This means the parties in
S_{p}
constitute a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
.
We note that all the edges which are present in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
when
S_{p}
constitute a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
are bound to be eventually
included in the graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
of every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
. This is because the edges are included by
P_{\ell}
based on various
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)

messages, which are received by
P_{\ell}
through various
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
instances. Consequently, due to the various properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
these
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)
messages are bound to be eventually delivered to every party in
\mathcal{H}
. As a result, all the properties which hold for
P_{\ell}
in the graph

G^{(\ell,\ell)}
when
S_{p}
constitute a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
are bound to hold eventually for every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
.
Since
P_{\ell}
is assumed to be
honest
, it computes the sets
\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
and
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, satisfying the following properties.

–

If
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
S_{q}
.

–

Else if
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
and

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set as
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.

–

Else
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is set to
S_{q}
and
q
is included to
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
.

We also note that if
S_{q}=\mathcal{H}
, then
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
and consequently,
P_{\ell}
will
not
make the share
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
public.
This is because
P_{\ell}
will set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
to
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
.
In more detail, the parties in
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
will constitute a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, since

S_{p}
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
, when
P_{\ell}
starts computing the core-sets
\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
will satisfy the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition, due to the
\mathbb{Q}^{(2,1)}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a})
condition.

Since
P_{\ell}
is
honest
, from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-weak validity
and
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network (see Theorem
3.2
),
it follows that all the parties in

\mathcal{H}
will eventually receive
(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the
broadcast of
P_{\ell}
. Moreover, each party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to the set
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.
This is because since
P_{\ell}
has computed the sets

\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
and
\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}

honestly
, these sets will eventually pass all the verifications for each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
.
The last statement is true because as shown above, all the properties which hold for
P_{\ell}
in the graph

G^{(\ell,\ell)}
when
S_{p}
constitute a clique in
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
are bound to hold eventually for every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
in the graph
G^{(\ell,\ell)}
.

The proof for the rest of the properties stated in the lemma is similar to that of Lemma
50
, except that we now rely on the security properties of ICP
in the
asynchronous
network; to avoid repetition we do not produce the details here.
The linearity of the underlying IC-signatures is ensured since the parties follow the linearity principle while generating the IC-signatures.

We next prove an analogue of Lemma
51
in the
asynchronous
network.

Lemma 55

If the network is asynchronous and if in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
any honest party
P_{i}
receives

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of any
corrupt dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, then all honest parties
P_{j}
will eventually receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}

and include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{j}
.
Moreover, for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
, the following holds, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
.

–

\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
. Moreover,

\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

–

If
q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then every honest
P_{i}\in S_{q}
will have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
.
Moreover, every honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
will have
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{j},P_{i},P_{k},{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q})
of every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for every
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Furthermore, if any corrupt
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
has
\mathsf{ICSig}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q})
of any honest
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
for any
P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}
, then

s^{\prime(\ell)}_{q}={s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
holds. Also, all the underlying IC-signatures will satisfy the linearity property.

Proof

The proof is very similar to the proof of Lemma
51
, except that we now rely on the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network.
Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}

be the set of
honest
parties. Now consider
an
arbitrary corrupt
dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and an
arbitrary

P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
, such that

P_{i}
receives

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})
from the broadcast of

P_{\ell}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
. Now consider another
arbitrary

P_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
, such that
P_{j}\neq P_{i}
.
From the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-weak consistency
and
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network, it follows that
P_{j}
is bound to eventually receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}
. We wish to show that
P_{j}
will eventually include
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{j}
.
For this, we note that since
P_{i}
has included
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
,

all
the following conditions hold for
P_{i}
,
for
q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
.

–

If
q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}
, then the set
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
.

–

If
(q\not\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p})
, then
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}
is either
S_{q}
or
(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, such that:

–

If
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=S_{q}
, then
S_{q}
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
.

–

Else if
\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}=(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
, then

(S_{p}\cap S_{q})
constitutes a clique in
G^{(\ell,i)}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}(S_{p}\cap S_{q},\mathcal{Z}_{s})
condition.

We claim that all the above conditions will hold eventually
even
for
P_{j}
. This is because
all
the edges which are present in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,i)}

when
P_{i}
includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
are bound to be eventually present in the consistency graph
G^{(\ell,j)}
.
This follows from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-weak validity,
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-fallback validity,
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-weak consistency
and
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-fallback consistency

of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network (see Theorem
3.2
)
and the fact that the edges in the graph
G^{(\ell,i)}
are based on
\mathsf{OK}^{(\ell)}(\star,\star)
messages, which are received through various
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
instances.

The proof regarding the IC-signatures is exactly the same as Lemma
51
and to avoid repetition, we do not produce the formal details here.

Finally, based on the previous two lemmas, we prove an analogue of Lemma
52
and show that
in an
asynchronous
network, all honest parties will eventually output a “legitimate”
set of parties
\mathsf{CORE}
.

Lemma 56

If the network is asynchronous, then in
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
,
almost-surely all honest parties eventually output a common set
\mathsf{CORE}
,
such that at least one honest party will be present in
\mathsf{CORE}
.
Moreover, corresponding to every
P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}
, there exists some
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}
, where
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}=s^{(\ell)}
for an honest
P_{\ell}
,
which is the input of
P_{\ell}
for
\Pi_{\mathsf{MDVSS}}
,
such that the values
\{{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}\}_{P_{\ell}\in\mathsf{CORE}}
are linearly secret-shared with IC-signatures. Furthermore,
if
P_{\ell}
is honest, then the adversary’s view is independent of
s^{(\ell)}
.

Proof

The proof structure is very similar to that of Lemma
52
, except that we now rely on the properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network and
Lemma
53
-
55
.
Let
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{a}
be the set of
corrupt
parties and let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{P}\setminus Z^{\star}
be the set of honest parties.
From Lemma
53
, except with probability
\mathcal{O}(n^{3}\cdot\mathsf{\epsilon_{ICP}})
, almost-surely all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually
have a common set of committed dealers

\mathcal{CD}
, where

\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
. Moreover, corresponding to every dealer
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{CD}
and every
S_{q}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
,
every party in
(\mathcal{H}\cap S_{q})
will eventually have a common share, say
{s^{\star}}^{(\ell)}_{q}
, which is the same as
s^{(\ell)}_{q}
, for an
P_{\ell}
.
We begin by showing that once the set of committed dealers
\mathcal{CD}
is decided, then almost-seurely, all the
|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}

and then all the
|\mathcal{CD}|
instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
will eventually produce some output, for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
.

We first claim that irrespective of way messages are scheduled and the order in which the parties in
\mathcal{H}
participate in various
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instances, there will be
some instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
corresponding to some
S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
, which will eventually produce output
1
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
.
For this, consider the set
S_{p}\in\mathbb{S}_{|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|}
, such that
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
(such an
S_{p}
is bound to exist since
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
also holds, as
\mathcal{Z}_{a}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{s}
).
If there exists
some

P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
which starts participating with input
0
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
, then the claim is true, because
P_{i}
participates with input

0
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
, only after receiving the output
1
from some other instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
, say
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q)}
.
And hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
), all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually obtain the output
1
from the instance

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q)}
, thus proving our claim. On the other hand, consider the case when
no
party has yet started participating with any input in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
.
Then corresponding to each
P_{\ell}\in(\mathcal{H}\cap\mathcal{CD})
, every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually receive

(\mathsf{CanCS},P_{\ell},S_{p},\{\mathcal{W}^{(\ell)}_{p,q}\}_{q=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|},\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p},\{s^{(\ell)}_{q}\}_{q\in\mathcal{BS}^{(\ell)}_{p}})

from the broadcast of
P_{\ell}

and includes
(P_{\ell},S_{p})
to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
(see Lemma
54
).
Since
\mathcal{CD}\setminus\mathcal{H}\subseteq Z^{\star}
and
Z^{\star}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
(due to condition
\mathcal{Z}_{s}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{a}
),
it follows that
every party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually have a set
{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}
(namely
{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}=\mathcal{H}
),
where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}

and where
(P_{\ell},S_{p})\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
for every
P_{\ell}\in{\mathcal{}A}_{p,i}
.
Consequently, each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
will eventually start participating in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}

with input
1
, if they have not done so. And from the
\mathcal{Z}_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (see Theorem
3.1
), it follows that
all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
eventually obtain the output
1
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,p)}
, thus proving our claim in this case as well.

Now from the above claim, it follows that every party in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually
start participating in the remaining
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instances for which no input has been provided yet (if there are any), with input
0
.
And from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, almost-surely,
these
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instances will eventually produce common outputs, for every party in
\mathcal{H}
.
As a result, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually compute a
q_{\mathsf{core}}\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\}
. Moreover,
q_{\mathsf{core}}
will be
common
for all the
parties in
\mathcal{H}
, since it corresponds to the least-indexed
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instance among
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,1)},\ldots,\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|)}
, which produces output
1
. And from the

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-security
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, each
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,\star)}
instance produces a common output for every party in
\mathcal{H}
.
We also note that
q_{\mathsf{core}}
will be indeed set to some value from the set
\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{Z}_{s}|\}
. This is because as shown above, the index
p
where
S_{p}=\mathcal{H}
always constitute a candidate

q_{\mathsf{core}}
.

We next claim that corresponding to
S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{Z}_{s}}
,
there exists a subset of parties
{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
, where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that corresponding to every
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
,
the ordered pair
(P_{j},S_{q_{\mathsf{core}}})
is eventually included in the set
\mathcal{C}_{i}
of every
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
.
Assuming that the claim is true, we next show that there always exists a set of parties
{\mathcal{}B}
where
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, such that the

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instance eventually produce output
1
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
, corresponding to every
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}
. For this, we consider two possible cases.
If any
P_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
has started participating with input
0
in any instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
, then it implies that for
P_{i}
, there exists a subset
{\mathcal{}B}_{i}
where

\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
and where corresponding to each
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{i}
, the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
has produced output
1
. Hence from the

\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (see Theorem
3.1
), all these
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances will eventually produce output
1
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
.
On the other hand, consider the case when
no
party in
\mathcal{H}
has started participating with input
0
in any instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
.
Then as per the claim, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually start participating with input
1
in the instances
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
. Hence from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-security
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
all the parties will eventually obtain the output
1
in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances, corresponding to every

P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
. Thus irrespective of the case, the set
{\mathcal{}B}
is guaranteed.
As a result, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually start participating
in the remaining
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
instances for which no input has been provided yet (if there are any), with input
0
.
Consequently, from the
\mathcal{Z}_{a}
-security
of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network (Theorem
3.1
),
almost-surely, all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will eventually have some output from all the
|\mathcal{CD}|
instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,\star)}
.
Moreover, the outputs will be
common
for all the parties in
\mathcal{H}
. Furthermore, the parties in
\mathcal{H}
will have a subset
\mathsf{CORE}
, which corresponds to all the

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances, which produces output
1
. Note that
\mathcal{CD}\setminus\mathsf{CORE}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
holds, since we have shown that the

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(2,j)}
instances, corresponding to the parties
P_{j}\in{\mathcal{}B}
will produce output
1
, implying
{\mathcal{}B}\subseteq\mathsf{CORE}
.
And
\mathcal{CD}\setminus{\mathcal{}B}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
holds. Now since
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{CD}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
and
\mathcal{CD}\setminus\mathsf{CORE}\in\mathcal{Z}_{s}
, it follows that

(\mathcal{H}\cap\mathsf{CORE})\neq\emptyset
, since the
\mathbb{Q}^{(2,1)}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Z}_{s},\mathcal{Z}_{a})
condition is satisfied.

We next proceed to prove our claim about the existence of
{\mathcal{}B}_{q_{\mathsf{core}}}
.
Since the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
has produced output
1
, it follows that at least one party from
\mathcal{H}
, say
P_{k}
, must have participated with input
1

in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}^{(1,q_{\mathsf{core}})}
